景気の底堅さとインフレ圧力の根強さを確認。貿易
赤字はAI関連を含む設備投資需要などを背景に拡
大。非製造業PMIは拡大圏で推移する一方、仕入
価格指数の上昇は企業のコスト負担増を示唆

ユーロ圏総合PMIは節目の50超を維持。小売売上
高も小幅増加。ドイツの鉱工業生産は持ち直したが、
製造業受注は大型案件の反動で減少。景気は緩
やかな回復を維持するも、持続力には不透明感
欧
州
ASEAN経済は国ごとにばらつき。ベトナムは輸出や
鉱工業生産の好調を背景に高成⾧持続。フィリピン
では食料品やエネルギー価格高騰が消費の重石に。
タイでも原油高を背景にインフレ圧力が強まる
APAC
賃金改善が続く一方、個人消費の回復に弱さ。実
質消費支出は前年比減少が継続。消費者マインド

も小幅悪化し、物価高への警戒が根強い。景気動
向指数は低下したものの、基調判断は改善を維持
日
本
先週（10/5～10/12）発表の主な経済指標
米国では金融政策運営の変化と財政懸念、欧州では財政不
安が金利上昇圧力に。欧州では⾧期金利上昇による景気下
押し圧力が、ECBの追加利上げの必要性を低下させる可能性
金利・コスト上昇などの逆風下でも、設備投資意欲は旺盛。家
計の節約志向や企業間の格差は残るものの、価格転嫁・賃上
げも継続しており、日銀の想定するシナリオに沿った動き
注目点② 日銀さくらレポートは企業活動の底堅さを再確認注目点①米欧で金利上昇。異なる背景と金融政策への含意

米国：中間選挙を目前に住宅市場は軟調、アフォーダビリティへの不満が共和党への逆風に
 住宅ローン金利は足元で再び上昇
― 借入コストの負担増により、足元の住宅ローン申請件数は減少
 物価上昇が生活コストを押し上げるなか、金利上昇による住宅取得環境の悪化も家計の不満を助長する要因に
― トランプ大統領の生活費への政策対応は約7割が「期待より悪い」と評価しており、支持率は低下基調
―11月に控える中間選挙では、アフォーダビリティへの対応が引き続き主要な論点

米国：金融政策・財政の両面から米長期金利に上昇圧力
 住宅ローン金利の基準となる米国10年債利回りは9月以降水準を切り上げ、足元（10/6時点）では5.3％近傍に
― 構成要素をみると、追加利上げ観測の高まりを受け、実質予想短期金利が上昇。ウォーシュFRB議長の就任以降、フォ
ワードガイダンスの後退を背景に、経済指標への金利の感応度が高まっている可能性
― 加えて、足元ではタームプレミアムも拡大。中間選挙を控えた家計支援策への思惑などから、財政懸念が意識されてい

る模様。トランプ大統領は10/2、Medicare加入者2,000万人超への90ドルの一時金支給を発表
5

欧州：長期金利上昇を受け、一部ECB高官は追加利上げの必要性低下を指摘
6
 フランスでは国内政治・財政面の不安から長期金利が上昇。イタリア・ギリシャなど周辺の高債務国にも波及
― フランス国債の対独利回り差は急拡大し、一時欧州債務危機（2012年）以来の水準に。その後、大統領選の有力候補と
目されるルペン氏（国民連合）が財政赤字を縮小する意欲を示し、拡大はひとまず一服
―ECBによる介入（国債買い入れ等）の可能性については、複数の高官が検討段階にないとの見方を示す
 長期金利の上昇は需要を抑制。ECB高官からは追加利上げの必要性が低下するとの見方も
― エネルギー高によるインフレ圧力と金利上昇による景気下押し圧力が併存。ECBの政策判断は一段と難しくなる可能性
仏長期金利上昇の経済影響（左）と直近のECB高官発言（右）
（出所）LSEGより、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

### Page 8

7
日本：金利・コスト上昇下でも企業活動は底堅く、設備投資意欲の強さを再確認
 10月のさくらレポートではAI関連需要の拡大とその裾野の広がりを背景に、企業活動の底堅さを確認
― 同一の企業コメントに登場するテーマの組み合わせを集計した共起分析では、「金利×設備投資」の組み合わせが増加。
金利上昇への意識が強まる一方、成長・省人化投資の旺盛なニーズは継続
 賃上げや資産効果が消費を下支えする一方、物価高を背景とした消費の二極化が継続
― 企業は価格転嫁を積極化するも、消費者の節約志向が根強く、商品競争力や顧客層によって転嫁余力に格差
⇒景気・物価は強弱入り混じるも、日銀の想定するシナリオに概ね沿った展開。12月利上げ見通しは不変
企業コメントの共起関係の変化
（注） 同一の箇条書きコメントに、双方のテーマのキーワードがある場合を1件として計上。
なお、テーマ間の因果関係を意味するものではない
（出所） 日本銀行「地域経済報告」より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

0 10 20 30

金利 × 設備投資
価格転嫁×企業収益
AI関連 × 設備投資
中東情勢 × 価格転嫁
節約志向 × 消費・販売
7月
10月
（件）
設備投資における金利負
担、企業収益における価
格転嫁の重要性を示唆
 AI関連需要は想定を上回るペースで拡大。半導
体製造装置に加え、工作機械や光ファイバー関連
製品など幅広い分野に波及
 金利・建設コストの上昇下でも、競争力維持に必
要な投資を継続。一方、採算悪化や調達コスト増
から投資を縮小・延期する動きも
設備投資・生産
 原材料・人件費の上昇を受け、企業の価格改定は
機動化。値上げ頻度を引き上げる企業がみられる
一方、消費者の価格感応度も高まる
 価格転嫁が進み賃上げ原資を確保できる企業が
ある一方、転嫁不足から利益を削って賃上げを続
ける企業も
価格転嫁・賃金
 株高による資産効果や賃上げを背景に、高額品・
高機能家電・旅行などの需要は堅調
 一方、物価高の長期化を受け、高年層を中心に生
活防衛意識が強まる。低価格品へのシフトや値上

げに伴う販売数量の減少も

世界経済：エネルギー・食料の供給ショックによる負担は、所得階層や国によって格差
 生活費高騰に伴うインフレの影響は所得階層により格差
― 食料・エネルギー支出の割合が高い低所得層は負担が大。IMFは低所得層に絞った所得移転が望ましい対応と指摘
 エネルギー・食料の供給ショックによる影響は国家間でも格差
― 先進国では、エネルギー供給ショックによる消費の下押し効果は低所得層で特に大きい
― 食料品への支出割合が高い新興国・低所得途上国では、食料供給ショックによる消費の下押し効果が相対的に大きい

 タイでは過去にも大規模洪水が発生。2011年には、日系自動車企業の部材調達が滞り、日本国内生産に影響した経験も
―2011年の洪水発生時には、工場の被災や物流停滞、人員不足が発生。タイの製造業稼働率は一時30％近く低下
― 当時浸水した工業団地では日系企業約450社が被災。タイ製部品の調達難により、日本でも完成車が一時減産に
 今次局面では、現状、主要拠点への直接被害は確認されておらず、一部工場の生産停止にとどまっている模様
―10月頭時点では各社、大規模な設備被害や減産は見込まず、日本の部材調達への影響も限定的となる見込み
― ただし、10月中旬にかけ更なる大雨が予想されており、操業停止の長期化や被害地域の拡大には依然警戒が必要

日本：個人消費は耐久財特需が剥落。今後は良好な所得環境を背景に緩やかに拡大
12
 8月の消費活動指数は、耐久財特需の剥落などにより、前年比＋0.9％と伸びが鈍化
― 耐久財消費は春先の自動車特需の反動から減少。食品などの非耐久財も、根強い節約志向を背景に弱含みが継続
― 底堅く推移してきたサービス消費も、台風・大雨や猛暑による外出控えの影響などから拡大ペースが鈍化
 今後は良好な所得環境が消費を下支え。個人消費は緩慢なペースながら、底堅く推移する見通し
― 実質所定内給与はプラス圏の伸びを維持しており、基調的な所得環境は良好。増加基調の購買力が消費を下支え
― ただし、インフレ率の再上昇で消費の回復ペースは緩慢。マインドの改善が一服したことも回復ペースを抑える要因に

（注） 旅行収支要因は、非居住者による国内消費（インバウンド消費）を控除し、居住者に
よる海外消費（アウトバウンド消費）を加えるための調整要因。アウトバウンド消費は
SNAの家計最終消費支出に含まれるが、GDPの計算上は輸入として控除される

日本：景気ウォッチャーは消費の緩やかな持ち直しを示唆も、家計・企業とも先行きには慎重
13
 家計動向（現状判断DI）は改善。9月の消費回復を示唆するも、回復ペースは緩慢
― 特にサービス関連の上昇が顕著。8月に増勢が一服したサービス消費は、大型連休や秋の行楽需要を受け持ち直し
― ただし、先行き判断DIは低下しており、個人消費全体でみれば緩やかな回復であることを示唆
 一方、企業動向は、現状・先行きとも慎重。景気回復の勢いは強くない
― 企業動向（現状判断DI）は製造業・非製造業ともに低下。AI関連など需要は堅調も、資材高などの供給制約が重石に

― 先行き判断についても、原材料高や金利上昇、中東情勢の不透明感などが重石となっている模様

日本も「インフレ・金利のある世界」へ。
本稿では下記3つの疑問を解説。
Q1. 足元までの金利上昇は「良い金利上昇」か「悪い金利
上昇」か？
Q2. 先行きの金利上昇の「質」を左右するポイントは何か？
Q3. 日本はどちらに向かうのか、何に注視していく必要がある
か？
問われるのは「良い金利上昇」か
「悪い金利上昇」のどちらに向か
うかで、成⾧戦略、技術革新、
財政規律、市場環境が論点と
なる。

市場焦點
美國
• Fed褐皮書顯示，美國經濟整體維持溫和擴張，10個地區經濟活動小幅至溫和成長，消費及企
業需求仍有支撐 ，但住宅市場偏弱 、消費者價格敏感度升高 。就業僅小幅增加 ，薪資溫和成
長；另一方面 ，能源、運輸、原物料及關稅成本持續推升企業支出 ，物價在多數地區溫和上
升，反映經濟韌性與通膨壓力並存，增加Fed政策判斷難度。
美伊衝突
• 美國８月底宣布對逾 60個伊朗相關實體實施制裁 ，試圖切斷伊朗的石油 、金融與貿易收入 。
但伊朗歷經數十年制裁後 ，已建立相對成熟的制裁規避網絡 ，但短期預期美國仍難迫使德黑
蘭屈服。對金融市場而言 ，若制裁未進一步衝擊伊朗石油出口 ，油價上行空間相對有限；反
之，若美國擴大次級制裁並波及中國等主要買家，則可能推升原油價格與全球通膨預期。

韓股
• 儘管市場波動劇烈 ，韓國企業在第⼆季度財報季的盈利前景仍在持續改善 ，在三星電⼦和 SK
海力⼠公佈業績後，MSCI韓國指數的全年每股純益預期被小幅上調 ，此前5至6月修正步伐一
度放緩，如今⼜重新加快，整體來看，今年韓國盈利預期累計上調幅度接近 190%，並推動整
個新興市場盈利預期提升約 37%，晶⽚需求指引依然強勁 ，隨著盈利預測持續上調 ，台灣市
場也從中受益。

美國製造業維持擴張，需求降溫與高成本壓力牽制擴張動能
• 新訂單降溫，製造業維持擴張韌性 ：美國8月ISM製造業PMI降至54.6%，較7月55.6%回落，但仍連續第8個月位於擴張
區間；新訂單降至 53.7%、訂單積壓降至 51.8%，顯示需求動能較前月放緩 ，惟生產指數仍達 58.3%，連續第10個月擴
張，運輸設備、機械及石油與煤炭等主要產業維持成長，製造業整體仍具韌性。
• 生產韌性延續，價格壓力仍偏高：就業指數降至51.2%，雖較前月走弱但仍處擴張區間；供應商交貨指數升至59.3%，代
表供應商交期進一步拉長 ，而價格指數維持71.1%，原物料價格已連續 23個月上升，鋼鋁、關稅及石化產品成本仍是主
要壓力來源。整體呈現「需求降溫、生產偏強、成本仍高」的格局，企業利潤與通膨傳導仍需觀察。

• 後市觀察：製造業景氣仍具韌性 ，但新訂單放緩與投入成本高檔並存 ，後續應觀察需求能否延續 ，以及鋼鋁、能源與關
稅成本是否進一步轉嫁至終端價格上 。投資布局可聚焦半導體 、電機電⼦及AI基礎建設等受惠於製造投資與 AI資本支出
的領域；債券則以中短天期及投資等級公司債為主，降低利率波動風險，以股債配置分散景氣與利率風險。
ISM製造業PMI關鍵分項指標比較

褐皮書揭美國經濟溫和成長，成本壓力增添Fed政策難度
• 經濟溫和擴張，消費與企業需求仍有支撐：Fed褐皮書
顯示，自7月初至8月下旬，10個地區經濟活動小幅至
溫和成長、2個地區持平，消費支出整體小幅增加 ，製
造業、部分服務業及金融活動有所改善 ，資料中心與
國防相關需求亦提供支撐；但住宅市場表現仍偏弱 ，
消費者對價格更加敏感 ，顯示經濟維持韌性但內需表
現分化。
• 就業增幅有限 ，成本壓力仍牽動通膨 ：就業整體僅小
幅增加，7個地區就業成長 、5個地區大致持平 ，薪資
多數維持溫和上升；然而能源 、運輸、金屬、石化原
料及關稅成本仍持續推升企業投入成本 ，部分企業甚
至難以完全轉嫁成本，整體價格仍呈現上升。
• 市場觀察： 褐皮書反映美國經濟尚未明顯轉弱 ，但就

業降溫與通膨壓力並存，使Fed政策取向更具挑戰。近
期市場對9月升息的預期明顯升溫 ，政策仍高度依賴後
續通膨與就業數據 。建議債券配置宜以短中天期美債
及投資級公司債為主 ，降低長天期利率波動；股票則
聚焦大型優質科技 、AI基礎建設及高現金流企業 ，並
搭配黃金分散通膨與地緣政治風險。

制裁使伊朗經濟受創，仍難迫使政權屈服
上海合作組織(SCO)佔全球經濟(GDP)的四分之一
資料來源：IMF，Bloomberg，2026.9.1，其他上海合作組織成員國包括巴基斯坦、伊朗、哈薩克、烏茲別克、
白俄羅斯、蒙古、吉爾吉斯、塔吉克和阿富汗資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.8.26
伊朗絕大部分石油銷往中國
• 制裁聲勢強，但關鍵仍在中國：美國８月底宣布對逾60個伊朗相關實體實施制裁，試圖切斷伊朗的石油、金融與貿易收入。
制裁能否真正奏效，關鍵仍在於是否將中國納入主要打擊對象。伊朗近八成的石油銷往中國，美方現階段僅將少數中港企業
列入黑名單，刻意避開了中國大型國有金融與能源巨頭，若強行實施全面制裁，可能危及關鍵礦產供應、破壞脆弱的美中貿
易休兵，甚至推升全球能源價格。

• 制裁使伊朗經濟受創，仍難迫使政權屈服：伊朗歷經數十年制裁後，已建立相對成熟的制裁規避網絡，並透過中國、俄羅斯、
印度及中亞國家參與的上海合作組織（SCO）等非西方平台維持經貿往來。俄羅斯與伊朗合作持續深化，巴基斯坦亦表態無
義務遵守美方制裁，印度則在制裁壓力下維持一定程度的經貿交流。因此，美國新一輪制裁雖將加劇伊朗經濟壓力，但短期
仍難迫使德黑蘭屈服。對金融市場而言，若制裁未進一步衝擊伊朗石油出口，油價上行空間相對有限；反之，若美國擴大次
級制裁並波及中國等主要買家，則可能推升原油價格與全球通膨預期，進而延後聯準會降息時程，增加股債市場波動。

歐元區通膨率升至近三年新高，９月升息機率高
• 歐洲央行9月升息機率高：歐洲央行（ECB）9月升息至2.50%的預期已逐步升溫，主因歐元區8月通膨升至3.3%，加上能源
價格受伊朗戰事推升，強化鷹派對進一步緊縮的主張。不過，核心通膨降至2.4%、服務通膨趨緩，且長期通膨預期仍大致穩
定，顯示能源價格尚未明顯向薪資與核心物價擴散，因此，9月升息後暫停仍是較可能的政策路徑。 9月升息後，ECB是否於
12月再次升息，將取決於能源衝擊的持續時間及其向核心通膨的傳導程度。
• 12月是否再升息，能源價格是關鍵：若伊朗戰事延續、油氣價格維持高檔，鷹派可能推動利率於12月進一步升至2.75%；反
之，若能源價格回落、服務通膨持續降溫，則本輪升息可能在9月告終。整體而言，ECB政策已轉向偏鷹，但在缺乏能源衝

擊廣泛傳導至核心通膨的明確證據下，年內連續升息空間有限。在政策利率逐步觸頂的情況下，歐債殖利率若因升息預期而
上行，反而可提供較佳的中長期配置與收益機會。
歐元區通膨（CPI） ECBspeak指數暗示歐洲央行還會進一步加息
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.9.2 資料來源：，2026.902

韓企業績展望在第⼆季財報加速改善
• 韓企業績展望在第⼆季財報加速改善 ：儘管市場波動劇烈 ，韓國企業在第⼆季財報的 獲利前景仍在持續改善 ，在三星電⼦和
SK海力⼠公佈業績後，MSCI韓國指數的全年每股純益預期被小幅上調 ，此前5至6月修正步伐一度放緩，如今⼜重新加快，整
體來看今年韓國獲利預期累計上調幅度接近 190%，並推動整個新興市場 獲利預期提升約37%，晶⽚需求指引依然強勁 ，隨著
獲利預測持續上調，台灣市場也從中受益。
• 展望後市：在韓國政府推動Value-up政策下，企業愈來愈重視股東權益 ，透過提高現金股利及擴大 買回庫藏股回饋股東，有
望成為第三季韓股上漲的重要動能，尤其是記憶體產業龍頭企業，擁有充沛的自由現金流，實施庫藏股買回，不僅能提升每股
價值，也反映管理層對未來獲利前景具信心，並認為目前股價被低估，參考日本推動公司治理改革後帶動股市重評價的經驗 ，

預期韓股後市仍具支撐力，若市場出現短期回檔，反而可視為逢低布局的機會。

阿里巴巴AI營收佳， 2027財年資本支出料將創下歷史新高
• 阿里巴巴AI營收佳，2027財年資本支出料將創下歷史新高 ：“AI+雲”基礎設施資本支出 ，將超過原定的3,800億元人⺠幣
的三年支出計畫，阿⾥巴巴將打造全棧式AI業務，CEO吳泳銘表示全棧AI商業化加速，預期雲增速持續加快，AI成核心增長
引擎，這不僅抵消部分盈利壓力，也凸顯新動能在結構調整中的價值，另外阿⾥巴巴自研GPU已實現規模化量產，並通過與
阿⾥巴巴雲業務及模型的協同設計 ，降低推理成本，最後是海外AI基礎設施目前仍在建設中，為這些詞元打開了中國以外的
市場，後續仍需觀察官方政策落地節奏與AI業務動能。
• 展望後市：隨著中國官方對科技產業態度轉趨友善 ，市場政策風險持續下降，同時晶片自主化與中國國產供應鏈發展仍是長

期戰略方向，相關受惠企業可望持續成長 ，另一方面科網龍頭的AI業務已逐步走出高投入階段，開始展現商業化成果與獲利
潛力，投資回報能見度提升，在政策支持、技術自主及商業化加速三大因素帶動下 ，AI概念股若因短期波動出現回檔 ，仍可
視為中長期布局機會。

美債市場結構性轉變：從不挑剔的機構到追求回報的私人投資者
• 自新冠疫情以來 ，美國10年期公債殖利率持續走高 ，過去市場多半歸咎於聯準會升息或通膨 ，但彭博最新研究顯示 ，期限
溢價的顯著上升才是主要推手，其背後的根本原因是美債買家發生了結構性轉變。
• 傳統非價格敏感型買家縮手：過去幾十年，聯準會(透過QE)與外國央行是美債的兩大底氣來源，他們買美債是為了儲備與流
動性，對價格不敏感，因此能有效壓低利率與期限溢價 。自2020年以來，聯準會與外國官方持有的美債佔美國 GDP比重分
別下降12%與8%，光是這兩大買家的退場，就推升10年期美債期限溢價約1.05%。
• 私人投資者接棒，市場對殖利率極度挑剔： 目前的邊際買家已轉為外國私人投資者 (如基金、保險公司等)，私人買家會精算

美債、公司債、股票與各國主權債的收益差異，進行嚴格比價。當美債發行量增加時，必須給予更高的殖利率才能吸引他們
購買，這使得美債利率波動更加劇烈。
自疫情以來，長期利率呈上升趨勢
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.9.1
主要機構買家持有的美國國債減少

供給端新挑戰：AI浪潮帶動公司債暴增，搶奪資金池
• 科技巨頭發債融資潮 ，公司債成為美債的強勁替代品： 受人工智慧建設資本支出帶動 ，大型科技企業 (如Alphabet、Meta、
Amazon等)積極發行投資級公司債 。這些高品質科技公司債提供優異回報 ，吸引了大量私人資金從美債轉向公司債 。為了
與這些高收益的高評級公司債競爭資金，美國國債殖利率面臨更大的上行壓力。
• 小結及建議：當前美債殖利率居高不下，核心原因在於穩定的買家減少，而接手的私人買家變得挑剔，加上AI熱潮創造了高
回報的公司債競爭源。在美國財政部發債壓力未減的情況下，長端殖利率短期內難大幅下行。債券策略建議採取配置中短天
期債券，長天期公債(10年期以上)價格受期限溢價與供給壓力壓制，波動較大，建議縮短持債年期(如布局3-5年期)，鎖定高

殖利率的同時降低價格波動風險 ，加碼高品質投資級公司債，目前私人資金偏好高評級公司債(尤其是科技與AI龍頭債)，這
類債券信用風險極低，收益率卻優於同天期公債，是當前固定收益資產的優質選擇。
外國私人投資者購買美國公債和公司債
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.9.1
人工智慧相關債務發行只是殖利率上升的原因之一

日圓再現匯率干預跡象 套利交易平倉恐加劇日圓升值
• 日圓再現匯率干預跡象：上週週間，日圓兌美元匯價出現單日大幅升值情況，市場再度懷疑官方出手干預匯率。然而，彭博
指出，日本央行帳戶數據沒有大規模買入日元跡象，意味著匯率的劇烈波動可能來自市場重新調整日本央行加息預期，而非
日本政府再次入市干預。與此同時，美國財政部並未回應是否參與干預。
• 市場懷疑干預之際，套利交易平倉可能正在發生：儘管無法證實官方上週是否直接干預日圓匯價，但日圓走升可能也反映上
週日本央行官員高田創釋出鷹派升息的態度，將不排除一次性大幅升息或連續升息的可能性。根據芝加哥商品交易所的數據
顯示，日圓融資的套利交易正在平倉，而且中期內持有日圓而非其他G10貨幣的意願非常強烈。
• 觀點：2024年8月日本央行意外升息，引發日圓套利交易平倉，全球主要國家股市短期間出現修正，當時AI與半導體成為套

利資金撤離重點。本次匯價波動暫時可歸咎於套利交易倉位的調整，尚未出現恐慌賣壓，重點將關注在本月中的日本央行利
率會議，留意意外大幅升息的風險。
資料來源：Bloomberg，截至2026.9.3。

• 今年年初黃金價格創下歷史新高，突破每盎司5,000美元大關，成為金融市場最受關注的資產之一，隨後經歷幾個月的高檔
修正後，上個月再度獲得市場資金追捧，現貨黃金價格單月大漲近10%。過去市場認為黃金上漲主要來自地緣政治風險與央
行降息預期，但近期投資邏輯似乎正在改變。
• 黃金多頭始自央行「去美元化」：本輪黃金多頭始自2022年烏俄衝突，西方國家凍結俄羅斯央行的大部分海外美元與歐元
外匯儲備，讓各國央行開始意識到外匯儲備的安全性，進而推動外匯儲備多元化。透過適度減持美元(美債)來佈局實體黃金
或其他國家貨幣，被成為「去美元化」趨勢。
• 「貶值交易」有望接棒黃金上漲動能：上個月美國公布最新國債規模已突破40兆美元，市場開始擔憂財政赤字的可維持性，
貨幣信心下降導致「貶值交易」發生(美債信心下降)，除了導致長天期美債殖利率上揚之外，貨幣避險需求再度讓黃金資產

成為市場焦點，8月份現貨黃金一度上漲超過15%，隨後雖因聯準會釋出鷹派訊號，單月漲幅仍接近10%。
貶值交易趨勢下，黃金的投資機會
全球央行黃金儲備佔比
資料來源：世界黃金協會、富蘭克林投顧，截至2025年，兆豐銀行整理。

• 央行買盤仍為黃金中期表現帶來利多：根據世界黃金協會2026年各國央行調查，上半年截至5/19，有45%的央行預期未來
一年將增加自身黃金儲備，創調查9年來新高。央行增持黃金主要考量包含：儲備多元化、美元走弱預期、地緣政治衝突以
及金價長期上漲趨勢，顯示黃金已成為各國儲備資產的重要戰略配置，在強勁的需求買盤下，多數機構預測黃金將維持多頭
至明年年底。
• 觀點：近期黃金價格反彈後，可能會受到獲利了結以及利率政策預期變化而出現波動，但在全球債務問題、貨幣貶值風險及
央行買盤支撐下，在中期內仍有機會走出慢牛行情。建議投資人以資產配置的角度，逢修正適度配置黃金資產(如：黃金ETF)。
風險接受度較高的投資人，則可關注金礦類股，但需注意其對於美元匯、利率的敏感度較高，資產價格波動較大。

1. 本資料內容僅供內部同仁及客戶參考，非作為任何金融商品或服務之購買或銷售之推介，不得視為買賣有價證券或其他金融商品之要約或要約之引誘。
2. 本資料提及之經濟走勢預測不必然代表基金之績效，基金投資風險請詳閱基金公開說明書。
3. 基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良管理人之注意義務外，不負責本基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益 ，投資人申購
前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
4. 本資料所載內容如涉及商品資訊 ，商品相關資訊仍以經核可之條款及文宣 (DM)為主，本行網站已備有公開說明書可供查閱基金應負擔之費用及相關投資風險 。投
資人申購基金係持有基金受益憑證，而非本資料提及之投資資產或標的。
5. 本資料所載內容取自本公司相信可靠之資料來源，且為特定日期之判斷，本公司不保證其即時性、精確性及完整性，亦不提供或嘗試遊說客戶做為投資之判斷依據。

資料內容邇後若有變更，本公司將不做預告或主動更新。投資人於決策時應審慎衡量本身風險，如因信賴本資料內容而做出或改變投資決策，須自行承擔結果，本
公司及本公司所屬員工，均不負任何損害賠償及其他法律上之責任。
6. 本資料內容之著作權屬兆豐銀行所有，禁止就所載資料任何形式之抄襲、引用或轉載。
7. 本資料所載部分基金涉及非投資等級債，投資人投資以非投資等級債券為訴求之基金不宜占其投資組合過高之比重 。基金經金管會核准，惟不表示絕無風險。由於
非投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，故本基金可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支
付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損。部分基金不適合無法承擔相關風險之投資人。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良
管理人之注意義務外不負責本基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
8. 基金年化配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額 ÷除息日前一日之淨值×一年配息次數×100%。「年化配息率」為估算值。當月配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額 ÷除

息日前一日之淨值×100%。
9. 基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動。投資人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績
效亦不代表未來績效之保證。本資料如以基金過去績效進行模擬投資組合之報酬率時，僅為歷史資料模擬投資組合之結果，不代表本投資組合之實際報酬率及未來
績效保證，不同時間進行模擬操作，其結果亦可能不同。
10. 部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之相關費用。基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部份，可能導致原始投資金額減損。各基金由本
金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至各投信基金公司及總代理人網站查詢。
11. 屬基金資訊觀測站ESG基金專區所列之基金，有關基金之ESG資訊，投資人應於申購前詳閱基金公開說明書或投資人須知所載之基金所有特色或目標等資訊。

Earnings revision: We factor in Landmark 3Q26 revenues and revise up earnings by 5%/ 
1%/ 1%/ 1% in 2026-29E mainly on higher revenues of CW laser epi-wafer and diodes 
for 1.6T products, supported by rising demand from high-speed optical modules. We 
keep GM and Opex ratio largely unchanged.

Valuation: We employ a discounted P/E methodology (unchanged) and apply a 61.5x 
(unchanged, report link) target P/E multiple on our 2029E EPS, discounted back to 
2027E at a COE of 10.5% (beta 1.7x, risk free rate 1.6%, and market risk premium at 
5.1% - unchanged). Our target P/E multiple of 61.5x is derived from the PEG&M ratio of 
peers (unchanged) in the photonics supply chain. Consequently, our 12-month target 
price is now NT$4,459 (vs. previously at NT$4,401). Our new TP implied 2028E P/E of 
76x is between company’s avg. P/E at 62x and avg. +1 P/E at 91x. Maintain Buy.

Price Target Risks and Methodology - LandMark Optoelectronics Corp.  
Valuation: We employ a discounted P/E methodology, applying a 61.5x target P/E 
multiple on our 2029E EPS, then discount it back to 2027E at a COE of 10.5% (beta 1.7x, 
risk free rate 1.6% and market risk premium at 5.1%), to derive our 12-month target 
price of NT$4,459. Our target P/E multiple is based on the same PEG&M ratio as peers in 
the photonics supply chain. We are Buy-rated on LandMark. 
Key risk: Slower-than-expected SiPh penetration; stronger-than-expected competition; 
Integrated Device Manufacturers (IDMs) offering Epitaxial (EPI) wafers in-house.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.
Coverage Universe: A list of all stocks in each coverage universe is available by primary analyst, stock and coverage universe at

https://www.gs.com/research/hedge.html.

Goldman Sachs is acting in an advisory capacity in a merger or in a strategic transaction involving this company, when there are legal, regulatory or
policy constraints due to Goldman Sachs’ involvement in a transaction, and in certain other circumstances. Early-Stage Biotech (ES). An investment

rating and a target price are not assigned pursuant to Goldman Sachs policy when this company has neither a drug, treatment or medical device that
has passed a Phase II clinical trial nor a license to distribute a post-Phase II drug, treatment or medical device.  Rating Suspended (RS). Goldman Sachs
Research has suspended the investment rating and price target for this stock, because there is not a sufficient fundamental basis for determining an
investment rating or target price. The previous investment rating and target price, if any, are no longer in effect for this stock and should not be relied

upon.  Coverage Suspended (CS). Goldman Sachs has suspended coverage of this company.  Not Covered (NC). Goldman Sachs does not cover this

TFT-LCD Panel Price Outlook
for Oct-26: TVs, Monitors and
NBs Flat MoM
While panel makers plan to raise the panel prices to mitigate
cost pressure, the coinciding softer seasonality in panel order
momentum makes this look a bit challenging, so negotiations
are undergoing. Contribution from class opportunities in
advanced packaging should only come in  2028 by earliest.

75" 4K 65" 4K 55" FHD 50" FHD 43" FHD 32" HD
Source:                         Trendforce, Morgan Stanley Research
TV panel prices to stay  flat MoM in October: Mainstream 32", 43", 55", 65" and 75"
TV panel prices are expected to stay flat for the month of October. While panel
makers had previously notified their brands customers of potential price hikes from
4Q26 to mitigate elevated component costs, we believe that negotiations are
ongoing, as TV panel order momentum might slow down in the coming months on

seasonality and TV brands could reduce orders on higher panel prices. On the other
hand, panel makers continue to demonstrate good discipline, since Chinese panel
makers are committed to annual maintenance during the October holiday to control
output. We think it seems more likely that we will see TV panel price hikes toward
late 4Q26 when TV brands start to restock for CNY promotions. Our base-case
assumption is for the TV panel prices  to be flat QoQ in 4Q26, on a quarterly average

basis.
IT panel prices to remain flat MoM in October: Monitor and notebook panel prices
are expected to be flat MoM during the month. Both applications continue to  see
slower demand in  2H26 post the earlier restocking  in 1H26 amid concerns about
increasing component costs. That said, panel makers are under cost pressure and
reluctant to given more price concessions. On a quarterly average basis, we expect
IT panel prices to remain flat QoQ in 4Q26.

Risk/reward  less attractive: We maintain the view that the current valuation of
Taiwanese panel makers, AUO (2409.TW) at 2.0x P/B and Innolux (3481.TW) at 1.7x
P/B, have already baked in the expectations of glass in advanced packaging for
revenue contribution in 2028. However, some supply-chain players think this might
take longer, and there seem to be more suppliers from different supply chains vying
for this opportunity. In China, while expectations are also building for advanced

packaging, we have a    relative preference for BOE (000725.SZ; 1.4x P/B) over TCL
(000100.SZ; 1.7x P/B), on its scale  and faster progress in glass core substrates.
Morgan Stanley does and seeks to do business with
companies covered in Morgan Stanley Research. As a result,
investors should be aware that the firm may have a conflict of
interest that could affect the objectivity of Morgan Stanley
Research. Investors should consider Morgan Stanley
Research as only a single factor in making their investment

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.
Key Takeaways
We believe current valuations of 1.5-2.0x P/B have already priced in expectations
of glass in advanced packaging from 2H28 for the panel makers.
Nonetheless,  the risk in commercialization and competition remain key to watch.

Morgan Stanley Research 3
TV Panel Prices Trended Down in 3Q26
TV panel prices started to edge up at the beginning of this year, rising 1% MoM in January,
2% MoM in February and 2% MoM in March. April, May and June were largely flat, while
July was down 2% MoM, August was down 1% MoM, and September was flat MoM,   in line
with our expectation that TV panel prices would start trending down from 3Q26.  That
said, we do not expect a significant decline as supply-side discipline should provide

downside support.

Below-seasonal Panel Shipments in 2H26
Over the past decade, TV panel shipments have typically been split 49%:51% between 1H
and 2H. In 2026, we expect the mix to skew toward 1H, as TV brands restocked ahead of
major promotional events, including the Winter Olympics in February, the FIFA World Cup
in June-July, and China's 618 shopping festival. We see no clear evidence that these events

will materially boost global TV demand, but they have historically shifted shipment timing
across quarters. As a result, we expect the restocking momentum seen since the start of
the year to fade, with panel order momentum slowing in 2H26.
Display Fab Utilization  ~85% in 3Q26
Stronger TV panel shipments supported display fab utilization of ~80% in 4Q24 and 82%
in 1Q25. Utilization declined to 78% in 2Q25 as brands adopted a more cautious stance
following earlier pull-ins and as the impact of China's trade-in program moderated.

Utilization rebounded to 80-85% in 3Q25 as brands restocked for year-end promotions
and remained at similar levels in 4Q25 despite seasonal softness as well as in 1Q26.
Industry utilization stayed broadly flat QoQ, at 80-85% in 2Q26, reflecting continued
supply discipline, followed by a slight uptick in 3Q26.
Over the medium to long term, we continue to believe the display industry is undergoing
structural change. Chinese panel makers have shifted their focus from top-line growth to

profitability, while lower government subsidies have reinforced supply discipline. We
believe investors increasingly recognize that the industry's production-to-order model can
remain sustainable, particularly as panel price cycles shortened to quarters in 2023-24 vs.
multiyear cycles in the previous decade. Consolidation actions, including LG Display's sale
of its Gen 8.5 Guangzhou fab to TCL and Sharp's closure of its Gen 10 Sakai fab, also

support a healthier supply backdrop (for more information, see Display – Structural
Changes on Supply Side Not Fairly Priced In, June 17, 2021).

Morgan Stanley Research 5
Risk/Reward Largely Fair
We view risk-reward as broadly balanced following the recent correction in panel stocks.
Below are our current views:
• AUO (2409.TW, Derrick Yang): We rate AUO Equal-weight with a PT of NT
$27.00, based on a 1.4x 2026e P/B multiple. While we believe the current round of
TV panel price increases is nearing an end, major panel makers should continue to
demonstrate solid supply discipline, providing downside support. We appreciate

AUO's efforts to diversify its business, but meaningful earnings contributions are
likely to take time.
• BOE (000725.SZ, Derrick Yang): We prefer BOE due to its scale advantage,
higher exposure to display and faster progress in glass core substrates. Our PT of
Rmb9.30 is based on 2.5x 2026e P/B, which we view as reasonable given our
estimated ROE of 5-8% over 2026-28 compared with BOE's average P/B of 1.1x and
ROE of 2-4% during 2023-25.
• Innolux (3481.TW, Derrick Yang): In semiconductors, our supply-chain checks

suggest that Innolux is participating in a foundry customer's glass core substrate
project. However, we do not expect meaningful contributions before 2028, and
additional competitors could enter as the industry moves toward mass production.
We maintain our Equal-weight rating and PT of NT$60.00, based on a 2.1x 2026e
P/B multiple.
• LG Display (034220.KS, Ryan Kim): Meaningful OLED revenue contribution
should increasingly improve earnings from 2H25, but potentially weaker end-

markets keep us from turning bullish on LGD. The stock trades at 0.6x 2026e P/B
vs. a historical mid-cycle level of 0.5x.
• TCL (000100.SZ, Derrick Yang): We are EW with a price target of Rmb4.70 (1.7x
2026e P/B). The stock is trading at 1.7x 2026e P/B vs. its mid-cycle average of 1.4x
since 2022.
• Greater China DDIC supply chain implications – Novatek (3034.TW, Daniel Yen):
Our Underweight thesis reflects weaker PC, smartphone and automotive demand,

gross margin pressure, and a lack of new growth drivers. That said, Novatek's
OLED DDI roadmap appears more competitive than those of its key Korean peers
in terms of both performance and cost, helping it gain share in Apple's iPhone
supply chain.

### Page 7

M
Idea
Morgan Stanley Research 7
Where We Could Be Wrong
• Stronger end demand: If end demand for major applications turns out to be better
than expected, there could be stronger panel shipments and less panel price
pressure.
• More stringent output control: If major panel makers collectively adjust
utilization to an extreme extent, they could mitigate pricing pressure in the case of
softer demand.
• Non-commodity display business: If the non-commodity business progresses

faster than expected, panel makers could show better financial results.

last 12 months. Due to rounding off of decimals, the percentages provided in the "% of total" column may not add up to exactly 100 percent.
Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over
the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.

Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated

below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -

relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

Growth in multiples
We forecast revenue to more than double next year
Bloom Energy’s planned capacity expansion of up to 10GW strengthens Kaori’s
long-term SOFC growth outlook, while higher liquid cooling content and
shipments should sustain liquid cooling momentum into 2027. CSP CDU heat
exchangers and semiconductor equipment cooling could provide additional

growth drivers from 4Q26. We raise our 2027/28 EPS forecasts by 14%/12% and
increase our target price to NT$2,710 from NT$2,300. Reiterate HC O-PF rating.
Bloom Energy to double its capacity
Bloom Energy (BE) has announced plans to acquire a 158k sqft facility in F remont,
U
S, comparable in size to its existing Fremont manufacturing plant, which can
produce up to 5GW. We believe the acquisition could support an eventual doubling
of its fuel cell stack manufacturing capacity to 10GW. We expect its supply chain

to support shipments of 2/4/6GW in 2026/27/28 to meet strong solid oxide fuel
cell (SOFC). demand. To support this ramp, Kaori is expanding capacity to 6-7x its
current level by end-2027. We now forecast Kaori’s sales from BE to grow by
185%/66% YoY in 2027/28, versus our previous estimate of 101%/77%.
Strong liquid cooling momentum into 2027
With SpaceX planning an additional 6-8GW of datacentre capacity in 2027, we
b
elieve GPU server suppliers Supermicro and Dell will be key beneficiaries,

supporting continued growth across their supply chains from 2H26 into 2027.
Higher content value for Rubin manifolds, together with stronger CDU and rear-
door heat exchanger (RDHx) shipments, should support Kaori’s liquid cooling
momentum. We forecast its liquid cooling revenue to grow by 60%/25% YoY in
2027/28.
Diversifying the growth engines
While SOFC and Supermicro-related liquid cooling remain Kaori’s main growth
d
rivers, we believe plate heat exchangers for CSP CDUs and liquid cooling

applications in semiconductor equipment could emerge as additional growth drivers
from 4Q26. These opportunities should broaden Kaori’s customer and application
exposure, supporting its longer-term growth outlook.
Earnings revisions and valuation
We raise our 2027/28SP EPS forecasts by 14%/12% to reflect higher expected
d
emand and shipments for BE's fuel cells, alongside a stronger liquid cooling
outlook. We increase our TP to NT$2,710 from NT$2,300, based on an unchanged

40x 2H27-1H28SP PE. Reiterate High Conviction Outperform rating.

Investment thesis
Kaori is well positioned to capitalise on rising demand for liquid cooling, and it
expects related product shipments to ramp up. The fuel-cell business should be a
huge growth driver in 2026-27 to meet AI power infrastructure demand in the US.
In the longer term, we believe the company will also benefit from heat-pump
demand as Europe is phasing out gas boilers, despite some noise about near-term
demand. Kaori has been planning capacity expansion earlier than peers to fully

capture demand, with rising exposure to large clients. We thus expect the company
to enjoy robust earnings in coming years.

### Page 5

Growth in multiples Kaori - HC O-PF
6 October 2026 5
Catalysts
The major growth catalysts largely depend on fuel-cell projects for AI datacentres
taking off, increasing content value in liquid cooling offerings, and a clearer outlook
for hydrogen electrolyzer development.
Valuation details
We adopt a PE-based valuation methodology as we believe the market will mainly
focus on the earnings growth outlook, which is better reflected with PE metrics.

We base our valuation on 40x PE, which is around +1sd of its five-year average and
supported by an 93% EPS Cagr in 2026-28SP, to account for the surging demand
for AI server liquid cooling and power infrastructure. We are positive on the
company's long-term outlook given its growing stationary fuel cell demand from AI
datacentres, as well as its efforts to diversify customer exposure for liquid cooling
business. We believe the company can maintain its competitive edge over other

cooling players given its expertise in vacuum welding technology to manufacture
high-quality products.
Investment risks
Downside risks: 1) increased competition for Bloom Energy's fuel cell business or
new technology solutions replacing fuel cells; 2) slower kick-off of new fuel cell
projects for AI datacentres; 3) weaker-than-expected liquid cooling adoption; 4)
slower rollout of Europe/US government subsidies for heat pump installations.

analyst’s coverage) will underperform and total stock return expected
to be below negative 10%. TSR is up/downside to 12-month target
price plus dividend. From 6pm (HK time) on 28 June 2024 and up to
16 December 2024: Outperform (O-PF): TSR expected to exceed or
be equal to 10%; Hold (HLD): TSR expected to be below 10% but more

BUY: Total stock return (including dividends) expected to exceed

but exceeding market return; U-PF (aka REDUCE): Total expected

### Page 1

매수 (유지)  
여전히 가격 상승 구간 
SK하이닉스의 26년 3분기 매출액은 95.8조원, 영업이익 75.1조원으로 예상한다. 
이전 전망 대비 감소한 수준이다. 원/달러 환율 영향이다. 영업이익률은 78.4%로 전 
분기 대비 2.1%p 상승할 전망이다. DRAM 매출액은 73.1조원, 영업이익 
59.2조원으로 추정한다. DRAM ASP는 이전 전망 대비 상향 조정했다. 일부 제품 
가격 인상을 반영한 영향이다. Bit Growth는 이전 전망 수준을 유지했다. ASP 
상향이 수익성 개선에 미친 영향은 크지 않을 전망이다. NAND 매출액은 22.4조원, 
영업이익은 15.9조원으로 예상한다. 
Global Top 수준 주주환원 규모/자사주 살 게 더 있다 
SK하이닉스는 2026년 8월 19일 주주환원에 대한 새로운 정책을 제시했다. FCF에서 
CAPEX를 제외한 금액의 50% 이상을 주주환원에 사용한다는게 핵심이다. 2026년 
기준으로 최소 90조원 수준일 것으로 추정한다. 자사주 중심으로 주주환원을 계획 
중이고 이미 집행 중인 40조원 이외에 4분기 중에 추가로 자사주 매입이 진행될 
것으로 예상한다. 배당도 일부 집행이 되겠지만 자사주가 중심이 될 전망이다. 
흔들리지 말아야 할 이유는 많다 
SK하이닉스에 대한 투자의견은 매수를 유지한다. AI 모델의 성장으로 메모리에 
대한 수요가 지속적으로 높아지고 있고, 이에 비해 공급 증가는 제한적인 상황이 
상당기간 지속될 것으로 전망하기 때문이다. 이로 인해 현재 수준의 DRAM, NAND 
가격에 변동성은 낮고, 높은 수익성이 유지되면서 안정적 현금흐름 창출이 가능할 
것으로 전망한다. 현재 주가는 지나치게 저평가되어 있는 국면에 있다고 판단한다. 
목표주가는 12개월 예상 EPS 446,092원에 PER 9 배를 적용한 4,000,000원을 
유지한다.

동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 외부의 압력이나 부당한 간섭없이 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하여 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
동 자료는 기관투자가 또는 제3자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다. 
동 자료는 조사분석자료 작성에 참여한 외부인(계열회사 및 그 임직원등)이 없습니다. 
조사분석 담당자 및 배우자는 해당종목과 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 
동 자료에 언급된 종목의 지분율 1%이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
당사는 상기 명시한 사항 외 고지해야 하는 특별한 이해관계가 없습니다.

2026.09.11  
HD 현대중공업 (329180)

드디어 엔진 증설, 저평가된 주가  Flash Note 
[조선/기계/방산] 변용진 2122-9181 yongjin.byun@imfnsec.com

Check Point  Buy (Maintain) 
HD현대중공업 중형엔진 및 SMR 공장 증설 결정  
엔진도 끌어올리지 못하는 조선업 주가, 그래도 펀더멘털은 굳건

목표주가(12M) 860,000 원(유지) 
종가(2026.09.10) 453,500 원 
상승여력 89.6 %

HD현대중공업, 중형엔진 및 SMR 시설투자 공시  
기다리던 엔진 증설의 세부 사항이 드디어 공개되었다. HD 현대중공업은 10일 공시를 통해 총 1조 722억원(중형엔진 
8,336억원/SMR 2,386억원) 규모의 시설투자 계획을 밝혔다. 먼저 중형엔진 관련, 글로벌 전력 수요 증가에 따른 엔진발전 시장 
확대에 대응 목적으로 생산능력 확충을 위한 토지 취득 및 공장 신설 및 생산설비 구축에 28년 5월까지 투자가 진행될 예정이다. 
중형엔진의 생산 능력은 현재 연간 3GW급에서 증설이 완료되면 7.2GW급으로 확장되며, 첫 엔진 출하는 28년 하반기로 예상된다. 
증설되는 신공장의 가동률은 28년 30%로 출발하여 30년 100%로 올라설 전망이다. 이에 따라 연간 1GW당 약 1조원의 매출과 
20%이상의 영업이익(현 엔진사업부 이상) 수준으로 추정되며, 계획대로 증설이 완료되고 일감을 확보한다면 총 4조원 이상의 매출 
증대 효과가 기대된다. SMR 투자 관련해서는 SMR 주기기 제작사업 진입 및 수주물량 증가에 대응하기 위해 SMR 전용 공장, 설비 
구축 및 기존 공장 최적화에 29년 4월까지 2,386억원이 투입된다. 이는 연간으로 Terrapower의 Natrium SMR 주기기 2기(각 
345MW)를 제작할 수 있는 규모다. HD현대중공업은 이번 증설이 데이터센터용 엔진만을 위한 것이 아님을 강조했다. 데이터센터 뿐 
아니라 국가전력망, 원전용 비상발전기 등 전세계적으로 증가하는 육상용 발전엔진 수요에 대응하기 위함이며, 최근 미국의 육상 
데이터센터 사업이 님비현상 등으로 일부 좌초되고 있음에도 그보다 더 큰 그림에 베팅한 것이다.  
엔진도 끌어올리지 못하는 조선업 주가, 그래도 펀더멘털은 굳건  
분명한 기업가치 증분의 근거가 되는 증설공시였음에도 불구하고 HD현대중공업의 주가는 발표 이후 하락으로 마감하였다. 올해 
조선업 주가는 YTD 평균 10%이상 하락하며 약세를 보이고 있는 상태다. (HD현대중공업 -10.0%, 삼성중공업 -11.6%, 한화오션 -
25.5%)  
조선사 공히 2분기의 실적 서프라이즈와 함께 올해 수주목표를 사실상 초과 달성하는 등 우수한 펀더멘털을 유지하고 있으나 이는 
주가 상승재료로 반영되고 있지 않다. 7~8월 반도체발 주식시장 혼란에 따른 전체적 수급 약세도 무시할 수 없는 요인이다. 여기에 
지난해 주가 상승의 주요 동력으로 작용하였던 미국 함정 사업 관련 협력 진전이 느린 점 등이 주가 약세의 주요 원인으로 
분석된다. 백악관 및 美해군에서는 지속적으로 함정 해외 건조의 필요성을 역설하고 있으나 美입법부에서는 지역구 의원의 반대 
등으로 관련 법안의 입법 동력이 약해져 있는 상황이다. 美국회의 이번 회기(119대 회기, ~2027년 1월 3일까지)가 이미 막바지에 
다다르고 있으며 11월 중간선거 일정까지 감안하면 실질적으로 이번 회기중에 관련 법안이 통과 및 발효될 가능성은 미미하기 
때문에, 2026년 남은 하반기에 함정사업 관련 조선업 주가 동력을 기대하기는 힘들어졌다. 다가오고 있는 3분기 실적도 원화 강세 
및 여름휴가/사고 등에 따른 조업일수 하락, 후판 유통가 상승 등 감익 요인이 산적해 있어 2분기에 비해 쉽지 않은 상황이다.  
그러나 여전히 본업인 조선업의 체력은 훌륭하다. 전세계 선박 발주량은 8월 누적기준 5,972만 CGT로 전년동기 대비 +60.6% 많은 
발주가 나오고 있으며 이는 2021년 이후 가장 강한 발주세다. 한국 조선사의 주력 선종인 LNG, 탱커, 컨테이너선의 발주 비중이 
전체 발주량의 67%를 차지하고 있으며 이에 힘입어 조선사들은 수주목표를 사실상 초과 달성하였다. (상선기준, HD현대중공업 달성 
130.6억 달러/목표 114.7억, 삼성중공업 달성 61억/목표 57억, 한화오션 달성 60억/목표없음) 3분기 실적은 다소 쉬어갈 수 
있으나 여전히 수주잔고에 기반한 추정상 매출/영업이익은 2028년까지 꾸준한 상승이 예상된다. 작년보다 오히려 하락한 주가가 
현저한 저평가일 수밖에 없는 이유다. 조선업 비중확대 및 HD현대중공업 Top Pick 의견을 유지한다.

Compliance notice 
당 보고서 공표일 기준으로 해당 기업과 관련하여, 
 회사는 해당 종목을 1%이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다.  
 금융투자분석사와 그 배우자는 해당 기업의 주식을 보유하고 있지 않습니다.  
 당 보고서는 기관투자가 및 제 3자에게 E-mail등을 통하여 사전에 배포된 사실이 없습니다.  
 회사는 6개월간 해당 기업의 유가증권 발행과 관련 주관사로 참여하지 않았습니다. 
 당 보고서에 게재된 내용들은 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
본 분석자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위한 참고자료이며, 따라서, 본 자료에 의한 투자자의 투자결과에 대해 어떠한 목적의 증빙자료로도 사용될 수 없으며, 어떠한 경우에도 작성자 및 
당사의 허가 없이 전재, 복사 또는 대여될 수 없습니다. 무단전재 등으로 인한 분쟁발생시 법적 책임이 있음을 주지하시기 바랍니다.

[투자의견] 
종목추천 투자등급  
종목투자의견은 향후 12개월간 추천일 종가대비 해당종목의 예상 목표수익률을 의미함.  
 Buy(매수): 추천일 종가대비 +15% 이상  
 Hold(보유): 추천일 종가대비 -15% ~ 15% 내외 등락 
 Sell(매도): 추천일 종가대비 -15% 이상 
산업추천 투자등급  
시가총액기준 산업별 시장비중대비 보유비중의 변화를 추천하는 것임 
 Overweight(비중확대) 
 Neutral(중립) 
 Underweight(비중축소)

economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.36 / 5 October 2026ECONEWS

ADVANCED ECONOMIES
EUROPEAN UNION / EUROZONE
Resilient corporate sentiment, upside inflation surprises. The Euro -
pean Commission’s Economic Sentiment Index for September showed
continued recovery in industry (+1.2 pts to -3.8, a 3.5 year high), with
a clear rebound in production and employment expectations, while

services improved further (+0.5 pt to 6.1). By contrast, household
confidence and sentiment in construction weakened further, as did
the composite index. These surveys broadly corroborate the PMIs,
with the September manufacturing PMI revised up by 0.2 pt to 52.9.
The unemployment rate held steady at a low 6.4%. Meanwhile, the
September flash inflation estimate came in above consensus at 3.8%.
The increase was driven mainly by energy (18.8% y/y, +4.5 pp) and

services (3.2%, +0.2 pp). Non-energy industrial goods inflation eased
slightly to 1.1% y/y, but the trend is firming: the 3m/3m annualised
rate is at a three-year high of 2.5%. Speaking before the data release,
ECB President Lagarde argued for a “measured response” and noted
that the increase in bond yields was delivering some of the needed
restraint. Tightening is also occurring via the cost-of-borrowing for
mortgage loans, which increased in August for the fifth consecutive

month to 3.6% (+6 bp m/m), a level unseen since September 2024.
It was unchanged for loans to non-financial corporations (3.8%).
Upcoming: ECB minutes (Thursday) and EU Trade Commissioner
Šefcovic's visit to Beijing (Thursday-Friday)
France. In the eye of the bond market storm. The government’s draft
budget for 2027, despite targeting significant adjustment (see our
detailed analysis), failed to stop the surge in the OAT yield, which
passed 4.9% on Friday morning (a high unseen since 2002), while the

OAT-bund spread reached a peak of 151 bps before easing somewhat
along with the global bond market selloff. (The peak coincided with
a statement by Bundesbank President Nagel that the ECB’s instru -
ments are focused on monetary policy transmission, not any spread
level). As expected, the draft budget law targets a deficit of 5% of GDP
(down from 5.4% in 2026), implying EUR 54 bn of savings, of which
about 2/3 coming from reduced spending. France’s independent fiscal

watchdog commended the spending moderation effort but pointed out
optimistic economic assumptions.
Meanwhile, inflation surprised on the upside: HICP inflation accele -
rated to 3.4% y/y in September (consensus: 3.1%), from 2.6% in August.
The sharp rise in energy prices (21.2%) was amplified by a 10% y/y
increase in the price of fresh food (linked to the summer drought).
Core inflation rose to 1.6% y/y in September, up from 1.3% in August
(services inflation at 2.2%; +0.3 pp m/m). The rise in industrial pro -

ducer prices accelerated to 6% y/y in August from 4.3% in July (+67%
versus +50% for refined products). Higher fuel prices also weighed on
household consumption, which fell by -0.5% m/m in August. On the
brighter side, the September manufacturing PMI was revised up by
0.3 points to 50.6.
Germany. Inflation accelerates to 3-year high. HICP inflation reached
3.3% y/y in September (vs. 2.9% in August and 3.2% expected) ac -
cording to the flash estimate (the highest since December 2023),

driven by energy prices. Core inflation rose by 2.4% (same as in Au -
gust) and services inflation edged down to 2.7% (vs. 2.8% in August).
The unemployment rate was stable in September at 6.4%.
Upcoming: August factory orders (Tuesday) and industrial production
(Wednesday)
Italy and Spain. Upside inflation surprises, business sentiment gains.
In Italy, HICP rose by 4.1% (vs. 3.2% in August), while core rose by 1.7%
(vs. 1.5% in August). In Spain, HICP inflation accelerated to 5% y/y

(vs. 4.6% in August), while core inflation reached 3.1% y/y (vs. 2.9%
earlier). Business sentiment, measured by the European Commission’s
ESI, rose slightly in both manufacturing and services in Italy, and
more significantly in Spain, where unlike in the rest of Europe, consu -
mer sentiment improved as well. Less positively, Italy’s unemploy -
ment rate unexpectedly rose to 6.2%.
UNITED KINGDOM
Bold new policy paths and upward growth revision. Prime Minister

Burnham broke several policy taboos in his Labour Party Conference
Speech, notably reconsidering Brexit and abandoning the so-called
“triple lock” guaranteeing that state pensions rise each year by the
highest of inflation, wages, or 2.5%. These proposals would be taken
up in the next Parliament. He also vowed to dismantle the legacy
of Thatcherism in public services and utilities. Real GDP growth for
Q2 2026 was revised upward to 0.5% q/q (vs. 0.4% previously, fol -

lowing 0.6% in Q1), driven by a stronger-than-expected performance
in trade and investment. Inflation expectations in the Bank of En -
gland’s Decision Maker Panel rose to 3.9% for own prices and 3.3%
for CPI one year ahead.
UNITED STATES
Solid growth, underwhelming payrolls. Q2 GDP growth was revised
up to 2.2% annualised (from 1.5%), with broad-based revisions to in -
vestment and consumption. The September ISM manufacturing in -
dex edged down (-0.1 pt to 54.5) on a drop in production. However,

new orders, a leading indicator, improved, and the employment in -
dex held at its highest level in four years. Payrolls rose by 29,000
in September, well below expectations, while the two months prior
were revised down by a combined 60,000. Job gains in healthcare
(+23k) and leisure/hospitality (+10k) were partly offset by losses in
Government (-10k). The unemployment rate rose to 4.2% (+0.1pp)
on higher labour force participation. Private hourly earnings growth

continued to slow (3% y/y, lowest since mid-2021). The August per -
sonal consumption expenditure (PCE) inflation report surprised to
the downside (3.4% vs. 3.7% expected), and core PCE was unchanged
at 3%. On the household side, the saving rate fell sharply (4.1% of
disposable income in August, from 4.6% in July), explaining the unex -
pected resilience of consumption (+0.6% in August inflation-adjusted).
All in all, this suggests the Fed will continue to raise interest rates

but doesn’t need to rush. We expect the next 25 bps hike in December.
Upcoming: September ISM services (Monday); FOMC Minutes (We -
dnesday).
JAPAN
Booming business sentiment. The Tankan survey index of large ma -
nufacturers rose to +24 (+2 pts q/q), while small and medium en -
terprises saw significant gains (+14; +5 pts q/q and +23; +5 pts q/q
respectively), driven by demand for semiconductors and production
equipment. Services sentiment weakened slightly (+35; -2 pts q/q for

large firms). Investment intentions remain strong among large cor -
porations and are showing signs of recovery in SMEs.

### Page 2

6
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.36 / 5 October 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
ECONEWS Find out more in our scenario and forecasts
EMERGING ECONOMIES
Manufacturing PMIs generally stronger. The index rose to 56.7 in
Taiwan (the highest level since 2021) and 53.9 in South Korea, boosted
by strong global demand for AI goods. Korean exports increased by
a record 83.5% y/y in September (of which chips: +263% y/y), mostly
led by export prices. The manufacturing PMI also improved and was

comfortably above 50 in India, Indonesia and Thailand. It declined
slightly in Vietnam (to 51.9). Malaysia and the Philippines stood out
with PMIs falling slightly below 50. PMIs also generally improved
in central Europe. In Latin America, PMIs were more mixed: falling
sharply in Brazil to 44.8, its lowest level since early 2023, but slightly
improving in Mexico (to 50) and Colombia (to 54.6).
ASIA
China. Better outlook on small stimulus and higher PMIs. Following

a string of data showing too sluggish domestic demand to meet the
Government’s growth target of 4.5 to 5%, the State Council announced
a package of stimulus measures, including the expansion of some re -
lending facilities aimed at financing tech/innovation, agriculture and
SMEs; new spending to be financed by carried-over local govern -
ment debt quotas; and a small interest rate subsidy on mortgages.
The latest PMIs published by the NBS and by RatingDog improved

marginally, with both now showing expansion in both manufacturing
and services.
EUROPE, MIDDLE EAST
Poland. Strong uptick in September’s inflation reading. Inflation
came in at 4.0% y/y in September (the highest level since June
2025) after 3.4% y/y in August, driven by higher prices at the pump.
The increase led the government to lower its VAT rate on fuel to 8%
(23% previously) from October until year-end, which should help ease
inflationary pressures. Overall, inflation may not significantly exceed

the Central bank’s target on average in 2026. The Central Bank may
nonetheless consider one rate hike by year end.
Türkiye. A financial scandal with limited macro impact. A Ponzi sche-
me collapse has impacted around 455k retail investors with assets
reportedly worth about 1% of GDP . The crisis was swiftly handled
by the authorities to prevent financial contagion. 131 funds and two
home-finance institutions involved in the scandal were suspended

and taken over by public banks for orderly and fair disposal of their
assets. Three investment banks and 2 factoring companies were
transferred to the Savings Deposit Insurance Fund. So far, 217 people
have been subjected to legal action, of whom 90 have been jailed
pending trial. Since September 9, the BIST-30 index lost 10% but is
still 25% higher than at end-2025, and foreign investors bought equi -
ties at the highest pace in 3 months last week, suggesting a buy-the-

dip reading of the situation. There has been no or marginal spillover
effects on the exchange rate, domestic interest rates and sovereign
spreads. At this stage, contagion risk to the banking sector is very low
but the damage to small investors may have negative spillovers on
consumption through negative wealth effect. Separately, the manu -
facturing PMI fell further in September to 47.9, signaling deepening
contraction.
Saudi Arabia. Budget deficit jumps on cost of war. Budget deficit is

now forecasted at USD 65.4 bn this year (-4.9% of GDP), up from
USD 44.3 bn (-3.3% of GDP) in the initial financing bill. Revenue is
expected to be slightly higher (+3.7%) as high oil prices more than
offset the decline in oil export volume. But spending has been revised
up by 9.3%. The new growth projection is also bearish, with GDP
expected to contract by -3.6% due to the decline in oil activity while
consensus sees economy growing by +0.2%.
LATIN AMERICA

Brazil. A tight runoff, a tough policy mix inheritance. Senator Flávio
Bolsonaro and Brazil’s incumbent President Luiz Inácio Lula da Silva
will face off in a runoff on October 25 th after neither won a majority
in Sunday’s general election (47% vs. 45% of the vote respectively).
Since Brazil returned to democracy in 1989, no candidate who trailed
in the first round has won the runoff. The next President will inherit
a slowing economy and a difficult policy mix: pressing ahead with

fiscal consolidation while normalising monetary policy amid recur -
ring supply shocks (adverse weather conditions, El Niño, war in the
Middle East).
ENERGY
Energy. Limited relief, electricity excepted. Re-escalation risks ap -
pear to be rising in the very short term in the Gulf and the Red Sea.
At the same time, the record high level of Gulf oil exports in Sep -
tember (above 80% of pre-war level including bypass and trade shutt-
le) has been enough to stabilize prices at a high level, albeit not to

put significant and lasting downward pressure on crude prices.
- Crude oil: prices declined slightly over the week but remain high
(-3.5% w/w to USD 101.6/b on Monday morning for Brent). The gap
with physical barrel prices (Dated Brent) is rising again USD 24.4/b on
Friday), signalling persisting tensions on the physical market.
- The diesel market got some relief from a G7 decision late on Friday
to release 100 mb of oil and diesel emergency stocks, focused on the

latter. It should take place over the next four months and reportedly
puts off the table US export restrictions. The European benchmark
fell markedly (-12% w/w to USD80/b on Monday morning). However,
market pressure could rise again soon as most Chinese refiners an -
nounced the suspension of fuel exports for October as the government
want to prioritize domestic supply security.
- EU wholesale electricity prices rose sharply in September, reaching

EUR 146/MWh on average, the highest level since Dec 2022.

Subject: Is FEMO Too Concentrated?
Date:    29 minutes ago
Is FEMO Too Concentrated?

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
Is FEMO Too Concentrated?
By Ed Yardeni & Elias Griepentrog • 13 Oct 2026
View in browser
Photo by Michal Balog / Unsplash

I. Stocks: Happy Fourth Anniversary!
Yesterday marked the fourth anniversary of the stock market bull run. The
S&P 500 is up 118.3% since it began. The bull market has survived the
pandemic, global supply-chain disruptions, rebounding inflation, two oil
price shocks, a significant round of Fed rate hikes, soaring bond yields, a
financial crisis, Trump's tariffs, and two wars. It has also survived concerns
about the market’s leadership being too concentrated in a few
outperforming stocks.

### Page 2

Only two of the S&P 500's 11 sectors have outperformed the index:
Information Technology (258.1%) and Communication Services (196.7%).
The others posted reasonably strong bull-market returns. Keep in mind
that the three most underperforming sectors include many companies that
pay relatively high dividends to shareholders (charts).

### Page 3

Since 2025, the current bull market has been increasingly led by FEMO
(Fabulous Earnings Momentum). Since the bull market started, S&P 500
forward earnings is up 73.9%, while the forward P/E is up 25.4% (chart).
A recent concern is that FEMO has been too concentrated. Our diffusion
indexes show that the percentages of S&P 500 companies with positive 3-
month and 12-month increases in forward revenues and forward earnings
have risen significantly during the bull market (charts).

### Page 4

On a y/y basis, all sectors are showing solid gains in forward earnings,
with the three worst laggards including many companies that pay relatively
high dividends to shareholders, as noted above (chart).

### Page 5

Nevertheless, this has been a tough year for stock pickers so far, with
41.2% of S&P 500 companies’ stocks down ytd (chart).
II. Update on the US Economy
As we noted recently, analysts expect positive revenue and earnings
growth across all 11 S&P 500 sectors in Q3 on a y/y basis. This broad-
based strength reflects the remarkable resilience of the US economy. The
latest economic indicators provide further evidence of that resilience.
Here's more:

(1) Bank earnings. The big banks kicked off Q3 earnings season today,
with JPMorgan Chase, Goldman Sachs, Citigroup, and Wells Fargo
reporting solid results. Their commentary confirms that strong consumer
spending and business investment are translating to robust lending growth

### Page 6

and investment banking activity, while credit quality remains healthy
(chart).
JPMorgan CEO Jamie Dimon pointed to "strong business investment and
ongoing consumer spending," while Goldman Sachs CEO David Solomon
observed that "the economy continues to be very, very resilient despite
some of the headwinds." Wells Fargo CEO Charlie Scharf offered a
particularly upbeat assessment, noting that despite mixed consumer
sentiment, "consumers are spending more, loan delinquencies are low,

and deposit and investment balances have grown." He added that strong
employment, wage growth, and AI infrastructure investment continue to
outweigh the risks from higher interest rates, oil prices, and inflation.
Citigroup CEO Jane Fraser likewise observed that despite all the attention
surrounding the AI boom, "the consumer is still driving much of the
growth."
(2) Consumer spending. The latest Redbook data confirm the bank
CEOs’ upbeat assessments of the US consumer. Redbook same-store

sales surged 10.4% y/y during the week of October 9, the strongest
increase since 2022 (chart).

### Page 7

Bank of America's September Consumer Checkpoint provides further
evidence of consumer strength. Credit and debit card spending per
household rose 6.2% y/y, or 5.1% excluding gasoline, both the third-
fastest rates in four years! Even more encouraging, spending growth is
broadening across income groups. Card spending rose 5.5% y/y among
lower-income households, compared with 4.9% and 4.8% among middle-
and higher-income households, respectively. Only the top 5% of earners

posted stronger growth, at 5.9%.
(3) Labor market. What's behind the broadening of consumer spending?
A key pillar is the labor market. Bank of America also reports that after-tax
wage and salary growth reached 4.5% y/y among lower-income
households in September, compared with 3.8% and 3.7% among middle-
and higher-income households, respectively. Stronger wage growth is
helping consumers absorb higher living costs without sacrificing
discretionary spending.

The latest weekly ADP data suggest that labor market strength persists.
Private-sector employment rose by 24,750 during the week of September
26, the sixth consecutive weekly acceleration and the strongest gain since
early June (chart). That implies a monthly pace of roughly 96,300 jobs,
comfortably above most estimates of the breakeven pace needed to keep
the unemployment rate low and stable.

### Page 8

Meanwhile, small business hiring plans moderated somewhat in
September but remained above historical norms. The latest NFIB survey
showed that 17% of small businesses planned to increase employment,
well above the historical average of 11% (chart). Job openings remained 8
percentage points above their historical average. A shortage of qualified
workers continues to constrain hiring, with 45% of firms reporting no
qualified applicants for their open positions compared with the historical

average of 37%.
The bad news is that the NFIB survey shows that pricing pressures
remained elevated in September (chart).

### Page 9

The most frequently cited problem faced by small business owners is the
quality of labor (chart). We expect they will solve this problem by boosting
productivity through AI and other technologies.
💡
Join the discussion with Ed below! To leave comments or questions,
log in to the Yardeni QuickTakes website and post them at the end
of the QuickTakes article. Paid members' contributions may be
featured in our segment, "Ed Answers Your Questions".

### Page 10

clause.

### Page 1

Sales Trader Commentary: TAIEX 49k Wobble -
July De Ja Vu, or Just Pausing Before the Santa
Rally? | GSTW
13 October 2026 | 4:11 PM Taiwan Standard Time
Robert Wang
Goldman Sachs (Asia) L.L.C. Taipei Branch
FICC & Equities
Index lost the 49k points threshold and saw the biggest decline on a point basis since
the end of July. Market seemed to lose the steam to break through 50k points after
the past choppy sessions. Is this July de ja vu or some healthy pullback? Foreigners

and domestic investors seem to share different narrative. Check out below for our
investor feedback.
Key Investor Feedback and Observations
Positioning. we saw meaningful outflows today from all kinds of investors today,
again focusing in ex-TSMC large caps. Today foreigners net sold US$5.2bn today,
the second largest in the history (simultaneously drove TWD weakness over 32.23
today). Domestic investors, especially retail, on the other hand, anchored the

market with the record high margin loan balance (as of 10/12/2026). Local funds
remained buyer as well mainly thanks to ETFs inflow.
Macro. CPI headline brought uncertainty and forced global investors to turn
cautious, which was exacerbated by high yields and crude. For Taiwan, AI trade is
relatively resilient to the Middle Eastern tension and high energy prices. However,
at some point, it is going to have a real economic impact either through Fed or

some other macro data.
Sentiment. Surprisingly, people are still bullish in Taiwan, though they also
acknowledge some choppy trading sessions ahead. Local investor feedback is not
calling for capitulation; in fact, most believe this is just another "two-steps forward
one-step backward" cadence towards another record high. Nonetheless, some
retail start taking chips off the table. This echoes our observation among the
foreign investors. Yes, the AI fundamental future is all sunshine, but as we head into

the end of year, it is natural for them to protect their great fun thus far this year
instead of adding on immediate risk. I'd imagine the next few weeks are going to be
full of 1) rapid sector rotation; 2) weak or unsustainable momentum before Santa
comes into town after the midterm election for our year-end rally Christmas gift.
TSMC Earnings and Others. We know the numbers are going to be good, and we
also know people are going to sell-on-facts after TSMC earnings call; recall most

recent one happened right after the meeting that scarred a lot of investors since.
Perhaps this time people started trimming ahead (and maybe too early?), which
provides some window for a quick rebound into small and mid cap. OCP summit is
happening right now, and TPCA Show will start next week. Watch out for single
stock movement mentioned in the conferences.
10/13/26, 4:13 PM Sales Trader Commentary: TAIEX 49k Wobble - July De Ja Vu, or Just Pausing Before the Santa Rally? | GSTW

https://marquee.gs.com/s/content/markets/812c043a-8535-4003-887e-b01f79096974 1/8

### Page 2

Sector. 1) ABF/CCL is still the crowded long sectors for both foreign and domestic
investor, but echoing on above points, investors need more catalysts to add on
risks or drive the re-ratings. 2) Power is another big issues that data centers need
to resolve, power solutions and fuel cells name are also key focus in Taiwan. 3) Let's
not forget about OSATs. We need more equipment and testing capacity to suffice
enlarging demand from both ASIC and GPU.

Key Stocks I Follow: Unimicron, Kinsus, NYPCB, ZDT, EMC, TUC, Delta, Lite-On,
Kaori, ASE, KYEC, All Ring
Risk. Upside looks thin in the short term. Good earnings are not enough - look at
Nanya Tech and Memory sector today. Chasing momentum now may get hurt. I also
smelled some short unwind today (i.e. funding short names outperformance such
as Realtek, Novatek, Shipper, etc) - signs of de-leveraging. Directionless is how I
would describe the past few days, but maybe it's time to find some value Buy?

Max loss amount invested is 100%

United States: Elevated inflation and high growth
02/10/2026 4
August 2026: Marked improvement in services
business confidence, from an already high level
September 2026: Manufacturing business

confidence remains stable at a high level
August 2026: Increasing inflationary pressures September 2026: Supplier deliveries do not get
longer
August 2026: Consumers are a bit more
concerned about their personal finances
August 2026: Headline inflation resists to the
upside
U.S. macroeconomic performance has remained solid since the onset of the shock, as illustrated in particular by Q2 growth
of +3.8% and +9% (annualized quarterly rate) in consumer spending and private nonresidential investment, respectively.

In the manufacturing sector, business sentiment has gained momentum despite the shock, while the impact of this shock
on the services sector has remained very limited. The immediate reaction of consumer confidence was, however, more
negative before beginning to improve, which was nevertheless interrupted in August (latest available data for households’
sentiment on their future personal financial situation). The University of Michigan’s composite indicator continued to

deteriorate in September, due to the sharp rise in fuel prices. But declining confidence still does not translate into reduced
spending—at least not at the aggregate level.
On the inflation front, the spike immediately following the outbreak of the conflict in Iran was more pronounced than in
the months following Russia’s invasion of Ukraine. The decline in June still left inflation at a high level, but its stability in
July and August was notable (3.4% y/y), as was the latest—the third consecutive—slight decline in inflation excluding

energy in August (2.5% y/y, same rate as in February). Given the existing inflationary pressures, however, inflation is highly
likely to resume its upward trend in September.
Source: BLS, ISM, NFIB, University of Michigan, BNP Paribas

Document 342: USA_ Pushing Back Fed Rate Hike on Lower Core PCE Inflation; Q2 GDP Revised Up to +2.2%; Trade Deficit Widens More Than Expected; Lowering Q3 GDP Tracking to +3.3%

Source PDF: all report pdf/USA_ Pushing Back Fed Rate Hike on Lower Core PCE Inflation; Q2 GDP Revised Up to +2.2%; Trade Deficit Widens More Than Expected; Lowering Q3 GDP Tracking to +3.3%.pdf
Report source: Goldman Sachs Publishing
Date: 2026-09-30

MAIN POINTS:
1. The core PCE index increased +0.25% in August, somewhat below expectations, and
the year-over-year rate increased 3.01%. We estimate that the Dallas Fed’s trimmed
mean PCE measure increased 0.16% month over month and 2.20% year over year.
2. Year-over-year core PCE was revised downward 0.36pp in July to 2.98%, primarily
reflecting the methodological revisions to the calculation of the portfolio management
(-0.23pp), computer accessories and software (-0.11pp), and legal services (+0.01pp)

components of PCE. These downward revisions were larger than we expected, mostly
reflecting a larger revision to the portfolio management component than we expected.
3. Today’s inflation report, especially the downward revisions resulting from the
methodological changes, imply that core PCE inflation is likely to substantially
undershoot the median FOMC participant’s forecast of 3.4% for 2026 on a Q4/Q4 basis.
We now expect a 3.0% increase for core PCE in 2026 on a Q4/Q4 basis. Coupled with

comments from New York Fed President John Williams yesterday that “with the policy
action we took at our September meeting, there is no need for urgency, and we have
time to gather more information,” we now think that an October hike is unlikely.  We are
pushing back the second hike in our forecast to December, and we see a strong chance
that the FOMC will ultimately conclude that additional rate hikes are unnecessary.
4. Real GDP was revised up by 0.7pp to +2.2% in Q2 (quarter-over-quarter annualized),

largely reflecting upward revisions to consumption (+0.4pp to +3.8%) and investment
(+1.9pp to +4.6%) growth. The strength in consumer spending in Q2 likely continued to
reflect the boost to consumer cashflow from larger-than-usual tax refunds, but we
continue to expect consumer spending growth to slow somewhat in 2026Q4 as the fiscal
support fades and the headwind from higher energy prices remains. Stronger
investment growth reflected an upward revision to private fixed investment (+0.5pp to

+9.0%), driven by data center investment in the nonresidential structures category
(+1.9pp to +0.1%), and an upward revision to the contribution from inventories (+0.2pp
to -0.5pp).
5. Reflecting today’s annual revisions to the national accounts, real GDP was revised up
by 0.4pp to +2.5% in Q1 (quarter-over-quarter annualized), largely reflecting upward
revisions to the contributions from consumption and exports. Revisions for prior years
were modest: real GDP growth on a Q4/Q4 basis was unrevised for 2025 (+2.0%) and

2023 (+3.4%), and slightly revised down for 2024 (-0.2pp to +2.4%) and up for 2022
30 September 2026   2
Goldman Sachs

### Page 3

(+0.1pp to +1.3%). 
6. Personal income increased 0.2% in August, below expectations. Year-over-year 
disposable personal income growth reached 4.8% in August. Personal spending rose 
0.9% in August, in line with consensus expectations. The savings rate decreased 0.5pp to 
4.1% in August, after a large 1.6pp upward revision to the savings rate last month driven 
by an upward revision to the level of disposable personal income (+1.2pp contribution) 
and a downward revision to personal spending as a share of DPI (+0.4pp). 
7. According to the Advance Economic Indicators Report, the goods trade deficit 
widened by $13.7bn to $132.6bn (seasonally adjusted) in August, well above 
expectations. Goods exports increased by $3.7bn to $203.4bn, largely driven by an 
increase in industrial supplies exports. Goods imports increased by $17.4bn to 
$336.1bn, driven by a large increase in industrial supplies and capital goods imports. 
8. According to the ADP report, private sector employment rose by 90k in September 
(month-over-month, seasonally adjusted), slightly above consensus expectations. 
Employment in the services sector rose 59k, primarily reflecting a 55k increase in 
education and health services and a 22k increase in leisure and hospitality employment 
that were partly offset by a 16k decrease in financial activities employment. 
Employment in goods-producing industries increased by 31k, driven by a 17k increase in 
manufacturing and a 15k increase in construction employment. ADP’s new median 
annual base pay measure, which better tracks other measures of wage growth than its 
gross pay measure, increased 3.0% for job stayers, unchanged from the August pace, 
and 4.8% for job changers, 0.1pp above the August pace. We left our forecast for August 
nonfarm payroll growth unchanged at +80k (vs. +90k consensus) ahead of Friday’s 
release. 
9. The details of the personal income and spending report were firmer than our previous 
GDP tracking assumptions. While the trade-related details of the Advance Economic 
Indicators report were much weaker than our previous assumptions, suggesting notably 
stronger imports growth in Q3 than we previously assumed, the inventories details were 
significantly stronger. On net, we lowered our Q3 GDP tracking estimate by 0.1pp to 
+3.3% (quarter-over-quarter annualized).

Document 349: What sector analysis tells us about Europe's recovery, two years after the _Draghi_ report (2026-10-12)

Source PDF: all report pdf/What sector analysis tells us about Europe's recovery, two years after the _Draghi_ report (2026-10-12).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-12
Pages: 2
Status: ok

### Page 1

The bank
for a changing
world
3
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.37 / 12 October 2026EDITORIAL
WHAT SECTOR ANALYSIS TELLS US ABOUT EUROPE'S RECOVERY, TWO YEARS AFTER THE "DRAGHI" REPORT
1 That amounts to EUR 800 billion to be committed in the EU by the end of the decade, which would bring defence budgets to 3.5% of GDP , up from 2.4% of GDP in
2026 (estimated figure). See Colliac, S. (2026), “Readiness 2030: One Year After Its Launch, the European Rearmament Plan Is on Track,” BNP Paribas EcoWeek, March.

2 Since 2025, critical metals have also been the focus of “strategic projects” for extraction in Europe, with the European Commission having just launched an ambi-
tious second wave, targeting lithium and cobalt in particular. See: Commission Selects 46 New Strategic Projects to Bolster the EU’s Supply of Critical Raw Materials
3 See Derrien G. (2026), “Next Generation EU: The Program Is Nearing Its End, but Its Impact on Economic Activity Will Endure,” BNP Paribas Chart of the Week, Sep-

tember 23.
4 NextGenerationEU Shows Strong Delivery Record as Implementation Reaches the Finish Line, October 7, 2026.
In 2026, the European economy will not perform better than it did in 2025. At 1.4% (IMF estimate), its growth remains half
as fast as in the United States. Still, there are more and more signs that Europe is regaining strength. The energy crisis,
which has now been ongoing for eight months, would have caused it to halt in other times. Beneath an apparent sluggish

activity, productivity is rebounding, while many sectors are picking up steam.
Insufficient growth and innovation, a technological lag, and an ex -
cessive dependence on China and the United States: two years ago,
the “Draghi” report painted a troubling picture of Europe and laid the
groundwork for a massive effort to revive the economy. Almost im -
mediately afterwards, the European Union (EU) adopted a “competi -
tiveness compass,” with the primary aim of strengthening its strategic

autonomy.
A 3D OUTLOOK: DEFENCE, DIGITALISATION, DECARBONISATION
Unsurprisingly given the geopolitical context, the most significant ini -
tiatives focused on defence: since March 2025, the SAFE (Security Ac -
tion for Europe) instrument has enabled the Commission to raise funds
on the markets (up to EUR 150 billion) to support member states in
implementing the “Readiness 2030” rearmament programme 1. Here,
the planning stage has long been surpassed, and disbursements for

the acquisition of military equipment are accelerating. According to the
EDA (European Defense Agency), European defence spending is projec-
ted to reach EUR 454 billion in 2026, already 30% more than in 2024.
The EU-27 have also set out to catch up in the field of artificial intel -
ligence (AI), committing up to EUR 200 billion in investments through
public-private partnerships. From fostering a culture of AI to promoting
and retaining talents, supporting startups, and sharing experiences and

data, the initiative is designed to be comprehensive. Its cornerstone is a
dedicated EUR 20 billion fund, co-financed by the Commission and the
European Investment Bank, which aims to establish five “giga-facto -
ries” across Europe capable of hosting and training the most powerful
AI models.
The EU is also leading the counteroffensive on other fronts, such as the
supply of critical metals, which it is seeking to secure through trade
agreements (most recently with Australia and South Africa) 2, and the

accelerated decarbonisation of the economy (with specific support pro-
vided to energy-intensive sectors). Its “Clean Industry Pact” is expected
to receive up to EUR 100 billion in funding from the Innovation Fund or
the European Union Emissions Trading System.
BENEATH THE SURFACE WINDS, SUPPORTIVE CURRENTS
In a report dated June 2026, the Institut Montaigne estimated that
nearly one-third of the recommendations in the “Draghi” report had
been implemented. Even before taking up the report, the EU had

already launched a significant investment effort through its NextGe -
nerationEU (NGEU) program: EUR 573 billion distributed via the
Recovery and Resilience Facility (RRF) between 2020 and 2026, with a
focus on energy infrastructure (particularly renewables), railway, di -
gital & connectivity 3. According to the Commission, two-thirds of the
6,000 “milestones and targets” assigned to governments have already
been linked to the RRF4.

### Page 2

4
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.37 / 12 October 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
What are the results on the ground? An analysis based solely on GDP
trends would not be conclusive: after a rebound in 2025, European
growth is projected to slow slightly in 2026. The International Mone -
tary Fund (IMF) estimates it at 1.4%, a rate nearly half that expected
in the United States.
In fact, the EU is showing remarkable resilience in the face of another

major energy shock that, in other times, might have pushed it into
recession. The German economy, one of the most exposed, is even gai-
ning momentum despite headwinds, a sign that, beneath the surface,
certain trends are gaining traction.
To assess the conflicting forces that have been shaping the Euro -
pean economy since the publication of the “Draghi” report, a sectoral
breakdown may prove useful. In particular, it allows to quantify the
ongoing transformation of the automotive industry, where a 150-year-

old technology — the internal combustion engine — is gradually giving
way to electric power.
TRANSFORMATION OF THE AUTOMOTIVE SECTOR
Year in and year out, European automakers are continuing their trans-
formation. After a lull in 2023-24 due to a decline in government subsi-
dies, European production of electric vehicles is picking up again (+30%
in 2025). In 2026, based on figures from the battery sector (production
up 25% y/y in July), this momentum continues. However, the volumes

involved (3.2 million electric vehicles assembled annually in Europe
compared to nearly 17 million in China, representing 75% of the global
market5) remain too low to offset the decline in sales in the traditional
internal combustion engine sector.
Compared to its pre-COVID average (2015-19), total European automo-
bile production has thus fallen by 30% in volume — a historic slump.
In 2026, against a backdrop of soaring fuel prices, the trend remains

negative (down 9.6% y/y in July, according to Eurostat). Upstream in the
supply chain, automotive suppliers, machine tool manufacturers, and
metal manufacturers are struggling (see Chart 1).
THE RISE OF AI
As an example of “creative destruction,” artificial intelligence is beco -
ming, alongside the energy transition, a driving force for the European
economy, as evidenced by the boom in related industries. In Germany,
the difficulties of the automotive sector stand in stark contrast to the

strong momentum in orders for electrical and electronic equipment.
In Europe, the production of IT equipment (computers, peripherals,
etc.) is growing at a rate of 40% per year — a level not seen since the
rapid digitalisation that accompanied the 2020-21 health crisis. Ge -
nerally speaking, most sectors involved in generating or transmitting
electricity (generators, transformers, cables, etc.) are currently outper-
forming the average. This is also true of the rail industry (locomotives

and other rolling stock), driven by “green” investment efforts under -
taken as part of the NGEU program.
5 Data from 2025. Source: IEA (International Energy Agency), Global EV Outlook, 2026.
6 See Derrien, G. (2026), ”When it comes to China, Europe should focus less on its trade deficits and more on its investments,” BNP Paribas EcoWeek, October 5.
DEFENCE EFFORTS
If there is one area where actions finally match commitments, it is
defence. Fighting vehicles, weapons and ammunition, as well as te -

lecommunications and optical equipment — which are ubiquitous in
drone systems — are all sectors where production volumes are brea -
king records and growth rates range from 20% to 50% annually.
***
Far from the image of stagnation it often projects, the EU has, for se -
veral years now, been making a significant effort to modernise and
strengthen the foundations of its economy. Although close to its end,
the NGEU programme will continue to support investment, while ini -

tiatives taken in the wake of the “Draghi” report are generating further
momentum. Sectoral analysis also shows that Europe is catching up
with the AI wave — admittedly belatedly, but with increasing momen-
tum.
Whether by coincidence or as a result, its hourly productivity has re -
bounded significantly over the past few quarters, returning to the 1%
annual growth rate it had before the pandemic (see Chart 2). Finally,
contrary to a fairly widespread belief, European companies have lar -

gely managed to maintain their competitiveness. Aside from the very
specific case of China, their trade in manufactured goods outside the
EU has tended to strengthen in recent years6.
Jean-Luc Proutat
jean-luc.proutat@bnpparibas.com

《醫療器械喜憂參半，但必要性手術需求仍佳》
近月醫療服務設備股為健護中股價表現較差 ，主因：
1)亞培受到產品責任險達成部分和解協議 ，後續還
有其他聯邦 、州判決仍為股價壓力； 2)波士頓科學
受資安事件影響生產宣告無法達成財測 ，HRS的左

心耳封堵術 (LAAC) 指南適用 WATCHMAN、 亞培
Amulet，可同時做消融+LAAC，但LAAC不能完全
替代口服抗凝血藥物 (OAC)對預防中風的效用 ，仍
為二線，等同喜憂參半 。另受自付成本增加 ，預期
擇期、外科手術需求疲軟延伸至年底 ，機器人手術
成長率區間接近 14.5%，下半年低於上半年 ，將以
擴大翻新系統的布署降低門檻及增加門診手術需求 ，
擴大目標市場 ，但必要醫療手術需求健康 ，心血管
類手術下半年仍成長，醫療器材基本面喜憂參半。
《星級獎金與醫療成本控管力使長期獲利分化》
每月聯邦醫療保險優勢計畫投保人數成長趨勢持續 ，
CMS預計於2026年10月中公布 2027年醫療優勢計
畫(MA)星級評等，因評級變嚴將影響醫保公司會員
留存率、競爭力及2028年品質獎金收入不確定性增
加，低於4星級業者面臨品質獎金減少與長期獲利承
壓風險。另無意外法案獨立爭議解決 (IDR)仲裁成本
持續推升商業保險理賠支出 ，增加2027年雇主團體
及ACA市場保費定價壓力 。管理式醫療業者今年展

現利潤優先於市占率、砍不賺錢計畫/人數拉抬利潤
率，但明年若保費調漲幅度不及醫療成本增速 ，恐
推升醫療損失率 、壓縮承保利潤 。後續獲利關鍵在
於星級評等 、保費調整能力及理賠成本控管 ，業者
間的長期獲利表現可能進一步分化。

以下，顯示市場過熱情況明顯改善 。然而在聯準會

格局。印度方面，9月回檔已使股價回測 3月低點，

美印經貿關係出現改善訊號 。後續可持續關注雙邊
合作進展及其對印度中長期投資影響。

《外資9月轉為小幅賣超，呼應8月轉中立看法》
8月下調東協評等至中立 ，主要考量 6至8月股市累
積漲幅已大致反映第三季經濟維持穩健成長的預期 ，
在缺乏新的催化劑下 ，市場進一步上行空間受限 。
進入9月後，美國公債殖利率與國際油價同步走升，
使市場重新評估高利率與成本壓力對企業獲利的影
響，並加大非科技類股及非科技市場的修正幅度 。
不過，相較於美國公債殖利率持續創高所帶來的壓
力，東協股市 9月僅下跌 2.6%，跌幅仍屬溫和；從
資金面來看 ，外資雖轉為淨賣超 ，但賣超規模明顯
低於第二季調整期間 ，顯示本波修正較偏向漲多後
的獲利了結與資產重新配置 ，而非反映市場對區域
基本面的明顯轉弱或出現恐慌性撤資。
重點摘要
• 東協9月受殖利率與油價上升

拖累回檔 ，但跌幅僅 2.6%，
外資賣超規模亦低於第二季 ，
顯示本波修正偏向漲多後的
獲利了結與資產重配置 ，而
非基本面惡化或恐慌性撤資 。
資料來源：Bloomberg ， 2026/9/30 ，中國信託商業銀行整理

《出口支撐成長敘事未變 ，但高通膨也比預期持久 》
東協及印度經濟仍展現韌性，多數國家8月出口維持
雙位數成長且高於長期平均 ，與全球製造業擴張趨
勢相符 ，其中新加坡及馬來西亞受惠 AI強勁資本支
出，8月出口成長年增率逾 5成，顯示外需仍是重要
成長支撐 。強勁的出口與製造活動不僅有助支撐企
業獲利 ，也在一定程度上抵銷高利率對消費與投資
的壓力 。另一方面 ，印尼、菲律賓等先前高通膨國
家雖已進入降溫趨勢 ，但通膨回落速度仍較緩慢 ，
使央行在降息時程上維持審慎 。整體而言 ，目前東
協與印度景氣尚未出現明顯轉弱 ，但高利率環境可
能維持較市場預期更久 ，意味短期經濟成長動能將
更仰賴外需及製造業活動的支撐。
重點摘要
• 東協及印度出口維持強勁成

長，反映全球製造業景氣仍
具韌性 ，並支撐區域經濟表
現；惟高通膨國家物價降溫
速度偏慢 ，恐使高利率環境
維持更久 ，短期成長仍須仰
賴外需支撐。
資料來源：Bloomberg ， 2026/9/30 ，中國信託商業銀行整理
高通膨國家通膨下降速度緩慢，低通膨的維持穩定

《川普表態支持印歐經濟走廊 ，重構印度樞紐角色 》
川普近期表態支持印歐經濟走廊 (IMEC)，使這項拜
登任內支持，但因加薩戰爭與區域衝突而停滯近3年
的計畫再度受到市場關注 。隨著中東地緣風險升溫 ，
紅海及荷姆茲海峽等關鍵航道不確定性提高 ，各國
對替代性能源與運輸路徑的需求同步增加 。從規劃
路線來看 ，印歐經濟走廊以印度為起點 ，串連海灣
產油國與歐洲市場 ，若計畫順利推進 ，除有助印度
取得更穩定的中東能源供給 、降低對俄羅斯石油的
依賴外 ，也將進一步鞏固其作為全球製造與供應鏈
樞紐的地位 。對印股而言 ，IMEC有望同時強化能源
安全與供應鏈地位 ，鞏固印度在全球製造的地位 ，
並為基建、工業及製造類股帶來中長期題材支撐。

重點摘要
• 川普表態支持擱置近 3年的印
歐經濟走廊重啟 ，在中東地
緣風險升溫下 ，有助印度強
化能源安全 、降低對俄油依
賴，並鞏固全球製造與供應
鏈樞紐地位。
資料來源：GettyImages

印度5月以來石油進口量已回到長期平均

熱門議題 / 投資關鍵
《AI代理將持續推動科技與半導體獲利成長》
根據機構LSEG統計，受到AI代理推動GPU、記憶體、
客製化AI晶片，以及相關硬體獲利持續高成長 。機
構上調科技產業 4Q26、1Q27獲利年增率到 53.7%、
51.6%，本行樂觀看待科技產業發展後勢。
受到AI代理需求大增與大量低價的開放模型加入 ，
帶動2026年Token使用量呈現指數成長 。根據大型
語言模型 API聚合平台OpenRouter統計，週Token
使 用 量 由2026 年 2-3 兆 Tokens暴增至 9 月 129 兆

Tokens，短短9個月成長超過 40倍，顯示AI代理帶
動AI推論指數級擴張 。另外，Meta於9月發表個人
AI代理Muse應用軟體，可替用戶「管理郵件、訂餐
廳、填寫表格 、比價網購 、安排日程」，用戶關閉
軟體後會繼續完成任務 。預計也將有眾多企業在
Muse平台使用自己的 AI代理。機構統計Muse在開
放前12天美加 iOS下載量達 180萬，高於ChatGPT
同期的130萬，這代表AI推論需求仍將供不應求。
市場投資展望
資產 4Q26看法 投資展望
科技 正向 AI代理帶動算力需求大增，半導體2027年將續增三成。軟體新增AI代理營收，資安前景持續看好。
• AI代理將帶動2027半導體成長
三成 Pg2
• 客製化AI晶片2027年成長高於 4
成 Pg3
• AI代理對軟體公司營收提供新動
能，資安成為AI 落地的先決條件

2
吸納量
重點摘要 《AI代理與推論將帶動2027年半導體成長三成》
受到AI持續推動對資料中心 、高效運算 GPU/CPU
與HBM、DRAM、NAND等記憶體強勁需求 。機
構WSTS統計2026年上半導體市場規模達到 7,020
億美元、成長102%。其中記憶體成長高達302%、
邏輯IC成長45%。所以WSTS於8月上調全球半導體
2026年銷售額到 1.66兆美元 、年增108%(機構6月
時預估 1.51兆美元 、年增90%)，也同步上調 2027
年銷售額至 2.14兆美元、年增29%(機構6月時預估
1.91兆美元、年增27%)。記憶體和邏輯IC合計將佔
83%的半導體市場。機構預估2027年半導體銷售額
仍將由AI資料中心需要的相關晶片帶動 ，包含記憶

體將年增36%、邏輯IC將年增21%。
《博通上修AI營收預估，雲服務商與AI模型商持續
加大投資客製化AI晶片》
博通FY26Q3( 2026/5-2026/7 )營收、EPS優於預
期，公司將2027財年包括客製化AI晶片( AI ASIC )
及AI網路的AI營收由1,000億美元上修至1,150億美
元，並預估2028財年AI營收將再翻倍至 2,300億美
元 ， 超越市場原先約 1,800 億 美 元 的 預 估 。
Anthropic和Google的TPU部署正按計畫推進 ，目
前博通已向Anthropic和Google大量交付了TPU v7
Ironwood，同時開始為 Google生產交付 TPU v8i，
預計本季將大幅提升交付 TPU v8i的規模。另外，
OpenAI和Meta的AI晶片專案也有所進展 ，公司已
推出OpenAI第一代客製化AI晶片Jalapeño。
• 機構上調2027年半導體市場
到年增29%
• 博通上修2027財年AI營收預
測，並預估2028財年AI營收

將翻倍至2,300億美元。
AI代理與推論持續帶動記憶體、邏輯IC大幅成長

吸納量
重點摘要 上述及後續專案將共同為博通 2027、2028財年AI
營收目標提供支撐 ，博通對未來AI營收的預估也顯
示雲端業者及 AI模型商正持續增加客製化 AI晶片投
資金額 。在客戶的 AI晶片部署進度上 ，博通預計
Anthropic在2026年將部署1吉瓦Ironwood系統、
2027年部署5吉瓦TPU v8i、2028年部署10吉瓦AI
晶片系統 ，預計Anthropic在2027年開始將成為博
通 最 大 客 製 化AI 晶 片 客 戶 ； 此 外， 公 司 也 預 計
OpenAI在2027年將部署 1.3吉瓦 Jalapeño、2028
年部署超過 5吉瓦Jalapeño及後續客製化 AI晶片。
由於市場預估客戶每部署 1吉瓦AI晶片系統約可為

博通帶來150~250億美元營收，根據博通公布的客
戶部署量，未來AI營收仍有上修機會。
《邁威爾上調 2027及2028財年營收預測 ，因市場
對資料中心互連、網路擴展產品需求強勁》
由於市場對資料中心互連 、擴展、客製化產品需求
強勁，邁威爾也上調了2027財年( 2026/2-2027/1 )
及2028財年( 2027/2-2028/1 )營收預測，將2027
財年營收預測由原先 115億美元上調至 120億美元
( YoY+45% )、2028財年營收預測由原先165億美
元上調至180億美元( YoY+50% ) 。邁威爾2028財
年成長潛力來自網路業務規模擴大 ，包括1.6T光模
組DSP、交換器，以及光學互連等新興需求；微軟
Maia 300、與Google的合作以及 XPU附加產品也
推升了2028財年客製化業務營收，市場對邁威爾AI
網通及客製化晶片項目的投資持續增加。
• 市場預估客戶每部署 1吉瓦
AI晶片系統約可為博通帶來
150~250億美元營收 ，未來
博通AI營收仍有上修機會。

零售客流量表
4
• AI 正從概念驗證轉向落地，並
產生可衡量的商業成果
《AI 正從概念驗證轉向落地，並產生可衡量的商業
成果》
9 越多家軟體龍頭不約而同揭露代理式 AI (Agentic
AI) 已由概念驗證階段跨入實質營收貢獻。益華電腦
(Cadence) 指出，其新推出的代理層採「消費加訂
閱」全新計費模式，且歷史經驗顯示效率提升不會
壓縮底層工具用量，一如 2006 年推出速度快 10 倍
的模擬器後客戶用量不減反增，原因在於晶片複雜
度呈指數成長，依台積電技術藍圖，未來 5 年晶片
複雜度將暴增48倍，人力根本無法同比例擴充。賽
富時 (Salesforce) 則與 Anthropic 共同推出
Claudeforce，並揭露頂級整合方案定價 550 美元，

較傳統業務團隊分散採購多套軟體之 1,300 美元支
出下降逾五成。近期財報數據顯示賽富時客戶加速
採用 AI 代理，AI 代理工作量加速成長並驅動相關
營收成長。MongoDB 的 Voyage 嵌入模型透過
Claude Code、Codex 等程式碼代理主動推薦，成
為全新的免費導流入口，單季即新增 2,900家 淨新
客戶；同時 MongoDB 也開始扮演前沿實驗室推理
記憶層角色，年化營收已達 5 億美元的語音 AI 獨角
獸 ElevenLabs 即因原生雲端資料庫無法承載代理
規模而轉單。Snowflake 則觀察到企業導入代理式
編碼工具後，資料遷移時程由過去以年為單位大幅
壓縮至一季內完成，帶動其由時間人力計價轉向成
果制計價的商業模式轉型。
